## GHSA-pwxh-7358-jq2x · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 8.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application used KaTeX to render mathematical expressions. When KaTeX encountered an error that wasn't a ParseError (e.g., a RangeError from deep nesting), it would fall back to displaying the original, unescaped user-provided content directly. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary HTML, including script tags, into the rendered output.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious client-side scripts into messages, leading to stored Cross-Site Scripting (XSS). This could allow them to steal user sessions, deface the application, or perform actions on behalf of other users.

### Key diff

```
-			renderedHTML = content;
+			renderedHTML = content
+				.replaceAll('&', '&amp;')
+				.replaceAll('<', '&lt;')
+				.replaceAll('>', '&gt;');
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the error handling for KaTeX rendering. If KaTeX fails to render content, the fallback mechanism now explicitly HTML-escapes the original content by replacing '&', '<', and '>' characters with their respective HTML entities before displaying it. This prevents the browser from interpreting any injected HTML as active content.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 104 occurrences**

## GHSA-3xpf-xq7r-v8c5 · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The application hardcoded 'allow-same-origin' in the sandbox attribute of an iframe used for file previews. This allowed an attacker to bypass the same-origin policy within the iframe if they could control the content displayed, leading to a same-origin XSS.

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary JavaScript within the context of the main application, potentially leading to account takeover or other sensitive actions.

### Key diff

```
-		sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-downloads{($settings?.iframeSandboxAllowForms ??
+		sandbox="allow-scripts allow-downloads{($settings?.iframeSandboxAllowForms ?? false)
 					? ' allow-forms'
-					: ''}"
+					: ''}{($settings?.iframeSandboxAllowSameOrigin ?? false) ? ' allow-same-origin' : ''}"
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the hardcoded 'allow-same-origin' from the iframe's sandbox attribute. It now conditionally adds 'allow-same-origin' only if a specific setting, 'iframeSandboxAllowSameOrigin', is explicitly enabled, thereby restricting its use by default.

## GHSA-3cg5-48j3-v4gv · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed a user with write access to a shared subfolder to delete the entire folder, including the owner's data, without proper authorization checks. The logic incorrectly assumed that write access to a subfolder granted deletion rights to the parent folder, which cascades to the owner's data.

### Impact

An attacker, who is a collaborator with write access to a shared subfolder, could permanently delete the original owner's chats and associated data by deleting the shared subfolder.

### Key diff

```
-            if user.role != 'admin' and not await _has_folder_access(user.id, folder, 'write', db):
-                raise HTTPException(
-                    status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
-                    detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.ACCESS_PROHIBITED,
-                )
+        if user.role != 'admin':
+            raise HTTPException(
+                status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
+                detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.ACCESS_PROHIBITED,
+            )
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the folder deletion logic to ensure that only the folder's owner or an administrator can delete a folder. It removes the previous complex checks for shared subfolders and simplifies the authorization to explicitly deny deletion by non-owners/non-admins, even if they have write access to a subfolder.

## GHSA-rq84-p6rr-vf89 · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The application's OAuth token exchange endpoint did not verify the `client_id` for which an access token was issued. This allowed an attacker to use an OAuth token issued to any client (e.g., a malicious client they control) to authenticate as the legitimate user on the Open WebUI platform, effectively bypassing the intended client restrictions.

### Impact

An attacker could perform an account takeover by obtaining an OAuth access token for a victim user from any OAuth client, then exchanging this token with Open WebUI to gain full access to the victim's account.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/routers/auths.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/routers/auths.py
@@ -1563,6 +1615,20 @@ async def token_exchange(
             detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.OAUTH_NOT_CONFIGURED(provider),
         )
 
+    if OAUTH_TOKEN_EXCHANGE_TRUSTED_CLIENT_IDS:
+        token_client_id = await get_token_client_id(client, form_data.token)
+        if not token_client_id:
+            raise HTTPException(
+                status_code=status.HTTP_400_BAD_REQUEST,
+                detail='Unable to determine which client the token was issued to',
+            )
+        if token_client_id not in OAUTH_TOKEN_EXCHANGE_TRUSTED_CLIENT_IDS:
+            log.warning('Token exchange denied: token was issued to an untrusted client for %s', provider)
+            raise HTTPException(
+                status_code=status.HTTP_403_FORBIDDEN,
+                detail=ERROR_MESSAGES.ACCESS_PROHIBITED,
+            )
+
     # Validate the token by calling the userinfo endpoint
     try:
         token_data = {'access_token': form_data.token, 'token_type': 'Bearer'}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new configuration `OAUTH_TOKEN_EXCHANGE_TRUSTED_CLIENT_IDS` to specify allowed client IDs. It also adds a `get_token_client_id` function to introspect the OAuth token and verify that the `client_id` it was issued for is present in the trusted list, preventing tokens from untrusted clients from being exchanged.

## GHSA-w2rx-84hp-gg95 · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's Playwright web loader did not properly validate URLs for sub-resource requests, including redirects. While the initial URL was validated, subsequent redirects were not, allowing an attacker to bypass the validation and direct the internal browser to arbitrary internal services or resources.

### Impact

An attacker could exploit this to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF), accessing internal network resources, cloud metadata, or other sensitive services that the Open WebUI application has access to.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
@@ -606,57 +606,63 @@ def __init__(
     def _intercept_navigation_sync(self, route, request=None):
         req = request or route.request
 
-        if req.resource_type != 'document':
-            route.continue_()
-            return
-
         try:
             validate_url(req.url)
+            resp = route.fetch(max_redirects=0)
+
+            if 300 <= resp.status < 400:
+                for _ in range(20):
+                    if not AIOHTTP_CLIENT_ALLOW_REDIRECTS:
+                        route.abort()
+                        return
+
+                    location = resp.headers.get('location')
+                    if not location:
+                        break
+
+                    url = urllib.parse.urljoin(resp.url, location)
+                    validate_url(url)
+                    resp = route.fetch(url=url, max_redirects=0)
+                    if not 300 <= resp.status < 400:
+                        break
+                else:
+                    route.abort()
+                    return
         except Exception:
             route.abort()
             return
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces URL validation for all redirect locations during navigation. It also explicitly blocks service workers and closes WebSocket routes in Playwright to further restrict potential attack vectors.

## GHSA-8x5v-cpv7-8jjp · open-webui

2026-08-04 · Python · **HIGH** 7.1 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's URL validation logic, which aimed to prevent access to private IP addresses, did not correctly identify and block NAT64-encoded IPv6 addresses that resolve to internal IPv4 addresses. This oversight allowed an authenticated attacker to bypass the intended security controls.

### Impact

An attacker could leverage this vulnerability to access internal network services, including cloud metadata endpoints, from the application's host, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure or further network compromise.

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
+++ b/backend/open_webui/retrieval/web/utils.py
@@ -111,8 +139,7 @@ def validate_url(url: Union[str, Sequence[str]]):
             # Check if any of the resolved addresses are private
             # DNS rebinding is mitigated at the connection layer; see _SSRFSafeResolver / _SSRFSafeAdapter
             for ip in ipv4_addresses + ipv6_addresses:
-                addr = ipaddress.ip_address(ip)
-                if not addr.is_global:
+                if not _is_global_addr(ip):
                     raise ValueError(ERROR_MESSAGES.INVALID_URL)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new helper function, `_is_global_addr`, which performs a more comprehensive check for global IP addresses, specifically accounting for various forms of NAT64 and other embedded IPv4 addresses within IPv6. This function is then used consistently across all URL validation and connection establishment points to ensure only truly global addresses are permitted.

## GHSA-cjc9-q5gf-327p · ghost

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 6.6 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the application did not properly validate or sanitize user-supplied filenames and theme names. This allowed an attacker to use path traversal sequences (e.g., '..') to write files outside of the intended directory or bypass theme name restrictions.

### Impact

An attacker could upload malicious files to arbitrary locations on the server's file system, potentially leading to remote code execution or other system compromises. They could also bypass theme name validation to create invalid theme entries.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ghost/core/core/server/adapters/storage/LocalStorageBase.ts
+++ b/ghost/core/core/server/adapters/storage/LocalStorageBase.ts
@@ -141,6 +141,13 @@ class LocalStorageBase extends StorageBase {
         const filename = await this.getUniqueFileName(file, targetDir);
 
         targetFilename = filename;
+
+        // Verify that we are saving directly under `targetDir` and not outside of it.
+        const expectedPrefix = path.join(path.resolve(targetDir), '/');
+        if (!path.resolve(targetFilename).startsWith(expectedPrefix)) {
+            throw new errors.BadRequestError({message: 'Cannot save to the given filename'});
+        }
+
         await fs.mkdirs(targetDir);
 
         try {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces two main fixes: it adds a check to ensure that the resolved target filename starts with the expected target directory prefix, preventing files from being saved outside the intended directory. Additionally, it adds a regular expression validation to reject theme names that consist solely of path elements like '.' or '..', preventing directory traversal in theme names.

## GHSA-g366-23fw-ggp6 · ghost

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.4 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application was fetching image sizes from URLs provided in Mobiledoc payloads without sufficiently validating the target URL. This allowed an attacker to supply arbitrary URLs, including internal network addresses, to the image size fetching function.

### Impact

An attacker could use this vulnerability to perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) attacks, potentially scanning internal networks, accessing internal services, or retrieving sensitive information from internal resources.

### Key diff

```
-                 } else {
-                     size = await imageSize.getImageSizeFromUrl(payload.src);
+                 } else if (storageUtils.isInternalImage(payload.src)) {
+                     size = await imageSize.getImageSizeFromUrl(payload.src);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an additional check, `storageUtils.isInternalImage(payload.src)`, to explicitly verify if the image source URL is considered an internal image before attempting to fetch its size. This restricts the URLs that can be processed by the `getImageSizeFromUrl` function.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 112 occurrences**

## GHSA-xm43-3m56-w3wf · ghost

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 5.3 · Pattern: `PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION→ROLE`

### Root cause

The Ghost platform allowed users to manipulate Stripe metadata during a donation checkout to simultaneously activate a gift membership. By setting both 'ghost_donation' and 'ghost_gift' metadata flags, the system would process the payment as a donation but still grant a gift membership, effectively allowing users to obtain paid memberships at a minimal donation cost.

### Impact

An attacker could obtain paid gift memberships for a significantly reduced price (e.g., 1 unit of currency) by exploiting the conflicting metadata flags during the donation checkout process, leading to unauthorized access to premium features.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ghost/core/core/server/services/members/members-api/controllers/router-controller.js
+++ b/ghost/core/core/server/services/members/members-api/controllers/router-controller.js
@@ -734,6 +754,8 @@ module.exports = class RouterController {
             metadata.newsletters = JSON.stringify(await this._validateNewsletters(JSON.parse(metadata.newsletters)));
         }
 
+        removeReservedCheckoutMetadata(metadata);
+
         const siteUrl = this._urlUtils.getSiteUrl();
         const successUrl = sanitizeReturnUrl(req.body.successUrl, siteUrl);
         const cancelUrl = sanitizeReturnUrl(req.body.cancelUrl, siteUrl);
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function `removeReservedCheckoutMetadata` to explicitly delete sensitive metadata keys ('ghost_donation', 'ghost_gift', etc.) from user-controlled input before creating a Stripe checkout session. Additionally, the webhook handler for `checkout.session.completed` now checks for conflicting metadata flags (`ghost_donation` and `ghost_gift`) and ignores sessions that have both, preventing the dual processing of a single payment.

## GHSA-jm22-3w23-5q7w · ghost

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 4.8 · Pattern: `INFO_DISCLOSURE→ERROR_MESSAGE`

### Root cause

The Ghost Admin API endpoints for posts and pages, including the export functionality, did not properly filter sensitive fields when retrieving data. This allowed authenticated users with access to the Admin API to potentially retrieve password hashes associated with authors when exporting or browsing post/page data.

### Impact

An attacker with access to the Ghost Admin API could obtain password hashes of authors, which could then be used for offline brute-force attacks to compromise user accounts.

### Key diff

```
--- a/ghost/core/core/server/api/endpoints/posts.js
+++ b/ghost/core/core/server/api/endpoints/posts.js
@@ -78,7 +79,11 @@ const controller = {
             unsafeAttrs: unsafeAttrs
         },
         query(frame) {
-            return postsService.browsePosts(frame.options);
+            const options = {
+                ...frame.options,
+                mongoTransformer: rejectAdminApiRestrictedFieldsTransformer
+            };
+            return postsService.browsePosts(options);
         }
     },
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `rejectAdminApiRestrictedFieldsTransformer` to the options passed to the `browsePosts`, `findPage`, and `export` methods. This transformer is responsible for filtering out sensitive fields, such as password hashes, before the data is returned via the Admin API.

## GHSA-2364-jh4q-m9vm · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application did not properly validate if the `customerId` or `subscriptionId` provided in API requests belonged to the authenticated user's active organization. This allowed an attacker to request information or perform actions on behalf of other customers/subscriptions by simply changing the ID in the request.

### Impact

An attacker could access sensitive customer or subscription details, such as payment source information, or manipulate subscription quantities and plans for other users without proper authorization.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/enterprise/controllers/organization.controller.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/enterprise/controllers/organization.controller.ts
@@ -80,6 +82,7 @@ export class OrganizationController {
             if (!customerId) {
                 return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Customer ID is required' })
             }
+            assertStripeIdMatchesSession(customerId as string, req.user?.activeOrganizationCustomerId)
             const identityManager = getRunningExpressApp().identityManager
             const result = await identityManager.getCustomerWithDefaultSource(customerId as string)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function, `assertStripeIdMatchesSession`, which verifies that the `customerId` or `subscriptionId` provided in the request matches the one associated with the user's active organization session. This check is now applied to all relevant API endpoints to enforce authorization.

## GHSA-3769-jgqc-cxm7 · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The application allowed custom `nodeVMOptions` to be merged directly into the `NodeVM` configuration without proper sanitization. This enabled an attacker to override critical security settings like `eval` and `wasm` within the `NodeVM` sandbox, which are designed to prevent arbitrary code execution.

### Impact

An attacker could escape the `NodeVM` sandbox and achieve Remote Code Execution (RCE) on the host system, leading to full compromise of the server.

### Key diff

```
-        const finalNodeVMOptions = { ...defaultNodeVMOptions, ...nodeVMOptions }
+        const finalNodeVMOptions = {
+            ...defaultNodeVMOptions,
+            ...nodeVMOptions,
+            require: defaultNodeVMOptions.require,
+            eval: false,
+            wasm: false
+        }
```

### Fix summary

The patch explicitly sets `eval: false` and `wasm: false` in the `finalNodeVMOptions` after merging custom options. This ensures that even if an attacker provides malicious `nodeVMOptions`, these critical security settings cannot be overridden, thereby preventing sandbox escape.

## GHSA-4j8x-x6v7-w9rq · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because user-supplied data, specifically the base64-encoded segment of a data URI in the CSVAgent's csvFile, was directly interpolated into Python source code without sufficient validation or sanitization. The `pythonCodeValidator.ts` file, which contained denylist-based checks for dangerous Python constructs, was completely removed in the patch, indicating that the previous validation mechanism was either insufficient or bypassed.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary Python code into the application, leading to remote code execution on the server where the Flowise application is running. This could allow them to take full control of the system, access sensitive data, or disrupt services.

### Key diff

```
--- packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
deleted file mode 100644
index 95a020b83e3..00000000000
--- a/packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
```

### Fix summary

The patch completely removes the `pythonCodeValidator.ts` file, which previously contained both allowlist and denylist-based validation logic for Python code. This suggests a fundamental change in how Python code is handled, likely moving away from client-side validation or removing the problematic code path entirely to prevent the interpolation of untrusted data into executable Python code.

## GHSA-52fh-8v99-63c2 · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The Python code validator used a denylist-based approach with regular expressions to prevent dangerous functions and modules. However, it failed to normalize Unicode homoglyphs and handle line continuations, allowing attackers to bypass the denylist by using obfuscated forms of forbidden keywords (e.g., `__cl\u{1D41A}ss__` instead of `__class__`).

### Impact

An attacker could execute arbitrary Python code within the Pyodide environment, potentially leading to full system compromise or data exfiltration, despite the intended sandboxing.

### Key diff

```
--- packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
deleted file mode 100644
index 95a020b83e3..00000000000
--- a/packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
```

### Fix summary

The security patch completely removes the `pythonCodeValidator.ts` file, indicating that the previous validation mechanism was deemed insufficient or replaced by a more robust, possibly sandboxed execution environment or a different validation strategy not visible in this diff.

## GHSA-5xvg-pmgg-3mxr · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from the removal of a Python code validator (`pythonCodeValidator.ts`). This validator was designed to sanitize user-supplied and LLM-generated Python code before execution in a Pyodide environment, specifically for CSV agent operations. Its removal allowed malicious Python code to bypass previous denylist and allowlist checks.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary Python code into the CSV agent's execution environment, leading to remote code execution (RCE). This could allow them to execute system commands, access sensitive files, or perform other malicious actions on the server hosting Flowise.

### Key diff

```
--- packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
@@ -1,390 +0,0 @@
-/**
- * Validates Python code before execution in Pyodide to prevent remote code
- * execution (RCE). Two entry points are provided:
- * ...
```

### Fix summary

The patch completely removes the `pythonCodeValidator.ts` file, which contained the logic for validating Python code. This indicates that the previous validation mechanism was deemed insufficient or problematic, and likely a more robust or different approach to securing Python execution is being implemented or was already in place elsewhere.

## GHSA-6vh2-wg4h-4vwj · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The application was using a spread operator (`...incomingInput.overrideConfig` or `...overrideConfig`) to merge an attacker-controlled `overrideConfig` object directly into the flow execution context. This allowed an unauthenticated attacker to inject arbitrary properties and potentially override critical configuration values or internal state.

### Impact

An unauthenticated attacker could manipulate the internal state and configuration of the flow execution, potentially leading to unauthorized actions, data manipulation, or denial of service by altering how the chatflow or agentflow operates.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/utils/buildChatflow.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/utils/buildChatflow.ts
@@ -562,8 +562,7 @@ export const executeFlow = async ({
         chatId,
         sessionId,
         chatHistory,
-        apiMessageId,
-        ...incomingInput.overrideConfig
+        apiMessageId
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the spread operator for `overrideConfig` from the flow execution context in `buildChatflow.ts`, `index.ts`, and `buildAgentflow.ts`. This prevents external input from directly injecting arbitrary properties into the internal configuration objects, thereby eliminating the property injection vulnerability.

## GHSA-c6xh-wv4j-ppv5 · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the IP address deny list mechanism did not properly normalize IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses (e.g., ::ffff:192.168.1.1) to their IPv4 equivalents before comparison. An attacker could use these special IPv6 formats to bypass existing IPv4-based deny list rules, allowing access to internal resources that should have been blocked.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the server-side request forgery (SSRF) protection, enabling them to make requests to internal network resources or other sensitive endpoints that were intended to be blocked by the deny list.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/components/src/httpSecurity.ts
+++ b/packages/components/src/httpSecurity.ts
@@ -57,7 +57,14 @@ export function isDeniedIP(ip: string, denyList: string[]): void {
     let parsedIp = ipaddr.parse(ip)
 
     // Normalize IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses to IPv4 before checking
-    // This prevents bypass of IPv4 deny list rules via ::ffff:x.x.x.x addresses
+    // This prevents bypass of IPv4 deny list rules via ::ffff:x.x.x.x addresses
     if (parsedIp.kind() === 'ipv6') {
         const ipv6Addr = parsedIp as ipaddr.IPv6
         if (ipv6Addr.isIPv4MappedAddress()) {
             parsedIp = ipv6Addr.toIPv4Address()
         }
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `isDeniedIP` function to normalize IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses to their IPv4 equivalents for both the input IP and the deny list entries. It also adjusts CIDR parsing for these normalized addresses and ensures consistent comparison for single IP entries. Additionally, it strips IPv6 brackets from hostnames before validation.

## GHSA-fm2f-4339-4p2f · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The application's execution update endpoint was not protected by any authorization checks, allowing any authenticated user to modify the public sharing status of any execution without proper permissions.

### Impact

An attacker could share or unshare any execution publicly, potentially exposing sensitive data or making private executions accessible to unauthorized users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/routes/executions/index.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/routes/executions/index.ts
@@ -8,7 +8,7 @@ router.get('/', checkAnyPermission('executions:view'), executionController.getAllExecutions)
 router.get(['/', '/:id'], checkAnyPermission('executions:view'), executionController.getExecutionById)
 
 // PUT
-router.put(['/', '/:id'], executionController.updateExecution)
+router.put(['/', '/:id'], checkAnyPermission('executions:update'), executionController.updateExecution)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new permission, 'executions:update', and enforces this permission on the execution update endpoint. UI components related to sharing/unsharing executions are also updated to only be available to users with this new permission.

## GHSA-fr6g-7cq8-fg82 · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's API endpoint for retrieving upsert history did not properly enforce tenant-level access control. It returned server-wide upsert history without filtering based on the authenticated user's active workspace or organization, allowing any authenticated user to view data belonging to other tenants.

### Impact

An attacker could access sensitive upsert history data from other organizations or workspaces within the same Flowise instance, leading to unauthorized information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/services/documentstore/index.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/services/documentstore/index.ts
@@ -613,7 +616,8 @@ const _normalizeFilePaths = async (
     appDataSource: DataSource,
     data: IDocumentStoreLoaderForPreview,
     entity: DocumentStore | null,
-    orgId: string
+    orgId: string,
+    workspaceId: string
 ) => {
     const keys = Object.getOwnPropertyNames(data.loaderConfig)
     let rehydrated = false
@@ -628,8 +632,15 @@ const _normalizeFilePaths = async (
         let documentStoreEntity: DocumentStore | null = entity
         if (input.startsWith('FILE-STORAGE::')) {
             if (!documentStoreEntity) {
+                if (!workspaceId) {
+                    throw new InternalFlowiseError(
+                        StatusCodes.PRECONDITION_FAILED,
+                        'workspaceId is required to resolve document store for FILE-STORAGE paths'
+                    )
+                }
                 documentStoreEntity = await appDataSource.getRepository(DocumentStore).findOneBy({
-                    id: data.storeId
+                    id: data.storeId,
+                    workspaceId
                 })
                 if (!documentStoreEntity) {
                     throw new InternalFlowiseError(StatusCodes.NOT_FOUND, `Document store ${data.storeId} not found`)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces new utility functions for tenant-scoped request guards, ensuring that data access is restricted to the user's active workspace and organization. Specifically, the `_normalizeFilePaths` function and related document store operations now require and utilize a `workspaceId` parameter to filter data by the user's active workspace.

## GHSA-gmmw-qg98-6j6p · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `IDOR→DATA_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application's Stripe subscription and customer management endpoints did not verify if the `subscriptionId` or `customerId` provided in the request belonged to the authenticated user's active organization. This allowed an attacker to manipulate billing information for other tenants by simply supplying their Stripe IDs.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially view or modify billing details, change subscription plans, or adjust seat quantities for other organizations within the Flowise platform, leading to unauthorized billing manipulation and financial impact.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/enterprise/controllers/organization.controller.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/enterprise/controllers/organization.controller.ts
@@ -62,6 +63,7 @@ export class OrganizationController {
             if (!subscriptionId) {
                 return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Subscription ID is required' })
             }
+            assertStripeIdMatchesSession(subscriptionId as string, req.user?.activeOrganizationSubscriptionId)
             const organizationUserservice = new OrganizationUserService()
             const totalOrgUsers = await organizationUserservice.readOrgUsersCountByOrgId(req.user?.activeOrganizationId as string)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new utility function, `assertStripeIdMatchesSession`, which is called in all relevant Stripe-related endpoints. This function verifies that the `subscriptionId` or `customerId` provided in the request matches the one associated with the user's active organization, preventing cross-tenant access.

## GHSA-p5w8-m249-4r4v · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTHZ→RESOURCE`

### Root cause

The application's DELETE /api/v1/chatflows/:id endpoint did not properly validate the type of resource being deleted against the user's permissions. While the route itself checked for 'chatflows:delete' or 'agentflows:delete' permissions, the backend service did not verify if the ID provided corresponded to a chatflow type that the user was actually authorized to delete, allowing users with 'agentflows:delete' to delete 'chatflows' and vice-versa.

### Impact

An attacker with delete permissions for one type of flow (e.g., agentflows) could delete other types of flows (e.g., chatflows) for which they did not have explicit delete permissions, leading to unauthorized data deletion and potential denial of service for specific functionalities.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/controllers/chatflows/index.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/controllers/chatflows/index.ts
@@ -76,7 +91,22 @@ const deleteChatflow = async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) =
                 `Error: chatflowsController.deleteChatflow - workspace ${workspaceId} not found!`
             )
         }
-        const apiResponse = await chatflowsService.deleteChatflow(req.params.id, orgId, workspaceId)
+        const userPermittedTypes: EnumChatflowType[] = []
+        const permissions = req.user!.permissions;
+        if (req.user?.isOrganizationAdmin) {
+            userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.CHATFLOW);
+            userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.AGENTFLOW);
+            userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.MULTIAGENT);
+            userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.ASSISTANT);
+        } else {
+            if (permissions.includes(`chatflows:delete`)) userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.CHATFLOW);
+            if (permissions.includes(`agentflows:delete`)) userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.AGENTFLOW);
+            if (permissions.includes(`agentflows:delete`)) userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.MULTIAGENT);
+            if (permissions.includes(`assistants:delete`)) userPermittedTypes.push(EnumChatflowType.ASSISTANT);
+            if (userPermittedTypes.length === 0)
+                throw new InternalFlowiseError(StatusCodes.FORBIDDEN, `You do not have permission to delete any chatflow types`);
+        }
+        const apiResponse = await chatflowsService.deleteChatflow(req.params.id, orgId, workspaceId, userPermittedTypes);
         return res.json(apiResponse)
     } catch (error) {
         next(error)
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a check within the deleteChatflow function to determine the user's permitted chatflow types based on their permissions. It then retrieves the chatflow to be deleted and verifies if its type is included in the list of types the user is authorized to delete. Additionally, the route now includes 'assistants:delete' in its permission check.

## GHSA-r745-8hwv-h473 · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The application allowed unauthenticated access to the OAuth2 token refresh endpoint. Additionally, the URLs used for OAuth2 authorization and token exchange (authorizationUrl, tokenUrl) were not validated, allowing an attacker to supply arbitrary URLs. This combination enabled an attacker to trigger server-side requests to internal or external resources.

### Impact

An attacker could perform Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) to access internal network resources, potentially exfiltrate sensitive information (like cloud metadata or internal service responses), or interact with arbitrary external services. Since the refresh endpoint was unauthenticated, this could be done without any prior access.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/routes/oauth2/index.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/routes/oauth2/index.ts
@@ -126,6 +127,15 @@ router.post('/authorize/:credentialId', async (req: Request, res: Response, next
             })
         }
 
+        try {
+            validateOAuth2Url(authorizationUrl)
+        } catch (err) {
+            return res.status(400).json({
+                success: false,
+                message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'Invalid authorization URL'
+            })
+        }
+
         const defaultRedirectUri = `${req.protocol}://${req.get('host')}/api/v1/oauth2-credential/callback`
         const finalRedirectUri = redirect_uri || defaultRedirectUri
 
@@ -246,15 +256,19 @@ router.get('/callback', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
             tokenRequestData.scope = scope
         }
 
-        const tokenResponse = await axios.post(tokenUrl, new URLSearchParams(tokenRequestData).toString(), {
+        const tokenResponse = await secureAxiosRequest({
+            method: 'POST',
+            url: tokenUrl,
+            data: new URLSearchParams(tokenRequestData).toString(),
             headers: {
                 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded',
                 Accept: 'application/json'
             }
         })
 
-        const tokenData = tokenResponse.data
+        if (tokenResponse.status >= 400) {
+            const errorHtml = generateErrorPage(
+                tokenResponse.data?.error || 'token_exchange_failed',
+                tokenResponse.data?.error_description || 'Token exchange failed',
+                tokenResponse.data?.error_description ? `Description: ${tokenResponse.data.error_description}` : undefined
+            )
+            res.setHeader('Content-Type', 'text/html')
+            return res.status(tokenResponse.status).send(errorHtml)
+        }
+
+        const tokenData = extractOAuth2TokenFields(tokenResponse.data)
 
-        // Update the credential data with token information
         const updatedCredentialData: any = {
             ...decryptedData,
             ...tokenData,
@@ -355,6 +369,15 @@ router.post('/refresh/:credentialId', async (req: Request, res: Response, next:
             })
         }
 
+        try {
+            validateOAuth2Url(tokenUrl)
+        } catch (err) {
+            return res.status(400).json({
+                success: false,
+                message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : 'Token endpoint URL is not allowed'
+            })
+        }
+
         const refreshRequestData: any = {
             client_id: clientId,
             client_secret: clientSecret,
@@ -366,19 +389,22 @@ router.post('/refresh/:credentialId', async (req: Request, res: Response, next:
             refreshRequestData.scope = scope
         }
 
-        const tokenResponse = await axios.post(tokenUrl, new URLSearchParams(refreshRequestData).toString(), {
+        const tokenResponse = await secureAxiosRequest({
+            method: 'POST',
+            url: tokenUrl,
+            data: new URLSearchParams(refreshRequestData).toString(),
             headers: {
                 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded',
                 Accept: 'application/json'
             }
         })
 
-        // Extract token data from response
-        const tokenData = tokenResponse.data
+        if (tokenResponse.status >= 400) {
+            return res.status(tokenResponse.status).json({
+                success: false,
+                message: `Token refresh failed: ${tokenResponse.data?.error_description || tokenResponse.statusText}`,
+                details: tokenResponse.data
+            })
+        }
+
+        const tokenData = extractOAuth2TokenFields(tokenResponse.data)
 
-        // Update the credential data with new token information
         const updatedCredentialData: any = {
             ...decryptedData,
             ...tokenData,
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces URL validation for OAuth2 authorization and token endpoints to ensure they are valid HTTP/HTTPS URLs and not internal IPs or reserved ranges. It also replaces direct `axios.post` calls with a `secureAxiosRequest` utility and extracts only specific, safe fields from the OAuth2 token response, preventing the storage of potentially malicious fields.

## GHSA-vmv7-4m6c-3cg5 · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The system allowed user-supplied Python code to be executed within a Pyodide environment. Although a denylist-based validator was in place to prevent dangerous operations, it was found to be insufficient. Attackers could bypass the denylist using Unicode normalization (NFKC) and line continuation characters, allowing them to inject and execute arbitrary Python code.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve full remote code execution on the server running the Flowise application, potentially leading to a complete compromise of the system, including access to sensitive data and the ability to install backdoors.

### Key diff

```
--- packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
deleted file mode 100644
index 95a020b83e3..00000000000
--- packages/components/src/pythonCodeValidator.ts
+++ /dev/null
```

### Fix summary

The patch completely removes the `pythonCodeValidator.ts` file, which contained the flawed denylist-based validation logic. This indicates a decision to remove the feature that allowed custom Python code execution, or to implement a more robust, possibly allowlist-based, validation or sandboxing mechanism elsewhere.

## GHSA-wg86-r78f-74mp · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `DESERIALIZATION→RCE`

### Root cause

The Flowise application allowed users to execute JavaScript code within a NodeVM sandbox. However, the sandbox configuration was not sufficiently restrictive, specifically regarding external dependencies and asynchronous operations. This allowed an attacker to bypass the sandbox and execute arbitrary code on the host system by importing powerful modules like 'puppeteer' or 'pg' and leveraging asynchronous functions.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve remote code execution (RCE) on the server hosting Flowise, leading to full compromise of the system, including data exfiltration, unauthorized access, or further network penetration.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/components/src/utils.ts
+++ b/packages/components/src/utils.ts
@@ -86,7 +86,6 @@ export const availableDependencies = [
     '@qdrant/js-client-rest',
     '@supabase/supabase-js',
     '@upstash/redis',
-    '@zilliz/milvus2-sdk-node',
     'apify-client',
     'cheerio',
     'chromadb',
@@ -1780,6 +1771,7 @@ export const executeJavaScriptCode = async (
             },
             eval: false,
             wasm: false,
+            fixAsync: true,
             timeout: timeoutMs
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch significantly restricts the allowed external dependencies within the NodeVM sandbox by removing several powerful modules (e.g., 'puppeteer', 'playwright', database drivers) from the `availableDependencies` list and the default `TOOL_FUNCTION_EXTERNAL_DEP` environment variable. It also adds `fixAsync: true` to the NodeVM options to better control asynchronous execution and introduces URL validation for `baseURL` in `ExecuteFlow` to prevent SSRF-like issues.

## GHSA-wp74-f5hh-5f3r · flowise

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The `/api/v1/files` endpoint lacked proper authorization checks for API keys. While it was intended to be restricted to certain plans via `IdentityManager.checkFeatureByPlan('feat:files')`, this check was commented out, effectively allowing any low-privileged API key to access it. Additionally, the endpoint was not included in the API key blacklist, further contributing to the authorization bypass.

### Impact

An attacker with a low-privileged API key could list and delete files across different workspaces within the same organization, leading to unauthorized data access and potential data loss.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/server/src/utils/constants.ts
+++ b/packages/server/src/utils/constants.ts
-export const API_KEY_BLACKLIST_URLS = ['/api/v1/nvidia-nim', '/api/v1/account/delete']
+export const API_KEY_BLACKLIST_URLS = ['/api/v1/nvidia-nim', '/api/v1/account/delete', '/api/v1/files']
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by adding `/api/v1/files` to the `API_KEY_BLACKLIST_URLS`. This explicitly prevents API keys from accessing this sensitive endpoint. The previous feature-based authorization check for this route was also commented out, indicating a shift to a more restrictive access control model for this specific endpoint.

## GHSA-x3hf-7cj6-3r4m · flowise-components

2026-08-04 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_WRITE`

### Root cause

The application allowed user-controlled input for SQLite database file paths without sufficient validation. This enabled an attacker to specify arbitrary file paths, including those outside the intended directories, leading to path traversal.

### Impact

An attacker could write SQLite database files to arbitrary locations on the server's file system, potentially overwriting critical files or placing malicious files in sensitive directories, which could lead to Remote Code Execution (RCE).

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/components/nodes/memory/AgentMemory/AgentMemory.ts
+++ b/packages/components/nodes/memory/AgentMemory/AgentMemory.ts
@@ -129,7 +134,7 @@ class AgentMemory_Memory implements INode {
 
         if (databaseType === 'sqlite') {
             datasourceOptions.database = databaseFilePath
-                ? path.resolve(databaseFilePath)
+                ? validateSQLitePath(databaseFilePath)
                 : path.join(process.env.DATABASE_PATH ?? path.join(getUserHome(), '.flowise'), 'database.sqlite')
             const args: SaverOptions = {
                 datasourceOptions,
```

### Fix summary

A new `validateSQLitePath` function was introduced to rigorously sanitize and validate user-provided SQLite database paths. This function checks for path traversal sequences (e.g., '..', encoded characters), null bytes, control characters, and ensures the resolved path is strictly within a set of allowed base directories.
